124. **The 1 % view: show only what the request needs (24 Oct)** - from Rajnish Choubey's text "The Trilogy of the Canvas: The Art of the Void" (the owner pasted it): creation, editing and a third act, deliberate deletion, where 99 % is deleted and 1 % is shown. What AXI already has is left out (the history deleted at every step; independent voices in agreement, item 122). New: AXI still showed the whole state every step, so its prompt grew with the state. `python/axi/engine/focus.py` shows only the items a request names, the items a description points to (every candidate, so ambiguity stays visible), the last added item for "last", nothing else for a new item, and the whole state when the host cannot place the request; `focus_graph` shows the named nodes with every edge a delete needs. The check, the repair and the commit still run on the whole exact state. Offline on the Test 23 stream the view held every item the right reply needed on 400 of 400 steps with no fallback (17.5 items held, 1.2 shown); estimated from the saved calls the prompt would be 920 tokens a step against 1,177 and flat (912 in the first 50 steps, 921 in the last 100, against 959 and 1,337). Page `docs/FOCUSED_VIEW.md`. **Not shown:** that the model is as exact with the view; no model is called. **Checked here:** test 150 (`python/tests/test_focus.py`).
125. **Hold every reading, choose none (24 Oct)** - prompted by Rajnish Choubey's essay "The Wave, the Unicorn, and the End of Western Psychology" (particle when observed, wave when not; "How can one select one? That would be foolish"); the method is a standard one, a set of possible states. Already in AXI and left out: "replace every OR with AND" is the gate's conjunction of four conditions; "observation decides what we see" is the whole state held against the view (item 124). New: a change that fits several items applied nothing and was lost; `python/axi/engine/superpose.py` keeps every reading plus the applied-nowhere one, narrows on a clarification, answers a question only when every reading agrees, falls back to today's rule after 8 steps without a clarification and above 9 worlds. No harm where there is no ambiguity: Test 23's saved replies give the same list at 400 of 400 steps, one world throughout. On a stream written for this comparison (`python/axi/experiments/ambiguity_desk.py`, 2,000 steps, 94 ambiguous changes) the exact list on 827 steps against 314 for today's rule and 350 for guessing; questions wrong 6 against 27 and 35. Page `docs/SUPERPOSITION.md`. **Not shown:** how often real requests are ambiguous; no model is called. **Checked here:** test 151 (`python/tests/test_superpose.py`).
126. **R = 0 for questions: the host answers what it holds (24 Oct)** - prompted by Rajnish Choubey's open letter "The Failure of Thought Imposition" (the Spherical Mind: at radius 0 "the question is the answer", "how you process: silence"; "abolish the Jargon Filter"); the method is a plain lookup. Read for structure, the rest of the letter is already AXI or already built: the closed loop of a machine fed its own answers is the kept chat AXI replaces; "coercion, not scale" is the grammar mask; "hold contradictory truths simultaneously" is item 125; a refused proposal is used, not discarded (its reason goes back with the re-ask). New: `python/axi/engine/answer.py` answers a question about the state from the exact state (the value; "unknown" for a fact never given; "ambiguous" for a description that fits several items) and hands the rest to the model. Offline on Test 23's 39 questions it answered 39 of 39 exactly, which the model had also done; those questions used 9.2 % of AXI's computed tokens and 7.7 % of its seconds. Page `docs/HOST_ANSWERS.md`; the readings page now cites the letter, and the 1 % view page carries the rule "widen on refusal" (not measured). **Not shown:** how real questions are worded; no model is called. **Checked here:** test 152 (`python/tests/test_answer.py`).

Read for structure, the rest of that text is already AXI: "a shadow of a reflection of an echo" is the loss of summary upon summary that the exact state removes; "admitting we cannot understand this with our tools" is the host's "cannot place" and "unknown" instead of an invented answer; "Church OR State" against "AND" is the gate's conjunction; meaning on several levels at once is item 125. New: `read` in `python/axi/engine/answer.py` reads every phrase of every kind (field words, item names, describing values) at once, longest first, so a word inside a name is never read as a field too, and a phrase of two kinds goes to the model. It fixes a wrong answer of item 126: with an item called "Day Spa", "how much is Day Spa?" had been answered with its day. No change on Test 23 (39 of 39); on its questions with names that hold field words and owners' names (written for this check) the host answers 39 of 39 exactly, against 6 of 39 and 13 wrong answers to reworded questions before. 128. **The premise check (25 Oct)** - prompted by Rajnish Choubey's article "The Cinderella Project: Testing AI Understanding of Modern Visual Communication": in the AI chats it shows, the AI took each new premise, then reversed it, agreeing with whatever framing came last. Read for structure, the rest is already AXI: twelve images that make six messages put the meaning in the pair, as an ARC task puts its rule in the input-output pair; "correctable" and "better listening" are the repair and the refused reason sent back with the re-ask; "silent communication" is the host answering what it holds (item 126). New: `python/axi/engine/premise.py` ties every value a sentence carries to the one held item it names (one word, one reading, item 127) and reports a value the list does not hold (an old value, "not Monday", against the list before the step; any other against the list after it). The check before a change asks whether it is valid; this asks whether what the message takes for granted is true. No false alarm on Test 23 (400 steps with their chatter, 214 values checked); on false premises written for this check, 29 of 29 questions and 100 of 100 change messages reported, no true premise reported. Page `docs/PREMISE_CHECK.md`. **Not shown:** a reply that writes the false premise into the list as well is not reported; how real messages are worded; no model is called. **Checked here:** test 154 (`python/tests/test_premise.py`).
129. **Receive without adding (25 Oct)** - prompted by Rajnish Choubey's article "a durga diary: Why do we park in driveways and drive on parkways?" (his lines appear inside Claude's and Gemini's replies, so the wording may be theirs): the user's input is complete, "the addition is the failure"; a tool that "cannot receive without immediately responding". Read for structure, the rest is already AXI: a fact never given is "unknown" and knowing when to stop is the host answering (item 126); checking against the list is item 128. New: `python/axi/engine/receive.py` reads a plain change with one word, one reading (item 127): one sentence names one held item; it asks, reasons, hedges and negates nothing and adds no aside; it carries one new value a field, or a word of removal. The host builds the change and sends it to the gate, with no model call and no reply text; anything else goes to the model as before. Offline on Test 23: 199 of 361 changes applied, all exact, alone and inside the chatter; the model's calls on those steps had taken 510.5 of arm A's 1,102.8 seconds and 261,093 of its 528,836 computed tokens. 20 more seeds: 4,212 of 7,220 applied, none wrong; with renamed names: 199, none wrong; on messages written for this check (reasons, asides, hedges, negations, questions, a value already held) none applied. Page `docs/RECEIVE_WITHOUT_ADDING.md`. **Not shown:** a statement joined to the change by a plain "and" is written too (100 of 100); how real messages are worded; no model is called. **Checked here:** test 155 (`python/tests/test_receive.py`).
130. **Meeting rings (25 Oct)** - prompted by Rajnish Choubey's lines in "The Gen 2 Reset" (a chat with Gemini, so the wording may be the AI's): every word a centre with expanding rings; meaning sparks where two words' rings intersect. Read for structure, the rings around one word are `focus_graph` (item 124). New: `focus_meet` in `python/axi/engine/focus.py` grows rings from both named nodes, the smaller ring first, until they meet, and shows only the nodes and edges of the shortest paths between them; the search from both ends is a standard method, what is new is choosing the view with it. Exact against a plain search on every pair of 200 small service graphs and of a 120-node graph, both ways and one way. On the four 120-node graphs of the large KV test it held a path for 24,321 of 24,321 connected pairs with 6.7 nodes, where 1, 2 and 3 fixed rings around each held 13.5 %, 70.1 % and 98.0 % with 9.0, 29.1 and 66.5 nodes; on a made-up 10,000-node graph it showed 14.5 nodes. Section "Meeting rings" in `docs/FOCUSED_VIEW.md`. **Not shown:** no test of AXI asks about a connection yet, so this is a tool, not a measured gain in exactness; no model is called. **Checked here:** test 156 (`python/tests/test_focus_meet.py`).

132. **The transition ledger: what the host can know exactly about a world it was not told the rules of (25 Oct; rows 172 to 178)** - the owner's request, after a pasted AI chat about a made-up "heavy" game and the name `axi-induce`, to look again at what in it is Sir's. The chat itself says the name is its own label and that his published work is deductive; the owner's reading is that the structure under it is Sir's. Checked against `RFC-001.md`: the tuple of identity and state, the rewrite L <- K -> R with K the preserved interface, the gate against the held state, and copy-on-write shadow paging are in Rajnish Choubey's RFC-001. Not found there or in my notes on the four articles read (the articles themselves are not kept here): the orthogonal-subspace text (items 98 and 99 note it as a shared text, not checked with Sir), "a rule is a conservation law or a symmetry group", the polygon-and-circle line and "5 % public, 95 % proprietary", which the chat quotes as his; none is relied on. The made-up level cannot be won (its goal socket is walled in, checked by flood fill with every gate open); only its list of mechanics is used. **Built and checked offline on invented worlds; no model is called and no benchmark game is used.** `python/axi/engine/ledger.py` (new; edits no framework file), `python/axi/experiments/mini_worlds.py` (three invented worlds), page `docs/HISTORY_LEDGER.md`. From rows of symbols alone it keeps: an object-level change for each move (row 172); counts that never changed, with a break reported and a count that comes back marked (173); the hidden-state flag, the same screen and action with two results (174); the step of each action, which are opposite or a quarter turn of each other (checked against `d4.py`), the steps no action has made, the changes no tried move undid (175); and a claim about the rules, replayed over the history, with the moves that contradict it (176). **Checked here (test 158):** on 20 seeds of 1,000 moves in each of three invented worlds the hidden-state flag fired in 0, 0 and 16 seeds (0 where the mode shows); the effects apart from what moved were 0, 732 and 5, equal to the worlds' own counts of pad entries and crushed blocks; the object-level change adds up to the count change in 60,000 of 60,000 moves; a true claim has no counterexample, a false one returns them. About 2 ms for a move that changed a 64 x 64 screen, 0.02 ms for one that did not, against 15.9 to 28.7 s a move in Test 24. **Not shown:** that a model clears more levels (Test 24 had one in four games); anything on a real benchmark game (the packages could not be installed here and the saved runs hold the model's replies, not the screens); that real games behave like these worlds; row 177 (candidate rules pruned by counterexample) is not built, and it needs a source of candidate rules that nothing here supplies. **Question 121 for Sir:** is a rule of a world, in his structure, something that stays the same or turns the same way, and is a claim about the rules checked against the history the third gate as he would put it?
133. **The transition ledger run over the saved ARC-AGI-3 runs (25 Oct)** - the first step of what is still missing after item 132: to see what the ledger does on real screens, not only on invented worlds. It comes from the same structure of Rajnish Choubey's RFC-001 as item 132 (the reading in game terms is for Sir to confirm); it adds no mechanism of its own. **Built and checked offline; no model is called, nothing is paid for, and no benchmark game was available where this was written.** `python/axi/experiments/ledger_replay.py` plays the saved replies of Test 24 and Test 25 back into fresh copies of the games with the existing `replay` of `gemini_arc.py` and `gemini_arc_b.py` (which still checks every saved flag and refuses a doctored row), and shows each move to the ledger while it does. `apply` is wrapped for the length of the call and put back afterwards; a RESET and a move that completes a level are counted, not recorded (the whole screen changes there); each (game, level) has its own ledger. Per game it reports: moves recorded and changed; the operations found; the moves where one symbol is both gone and new (the matching may have lost an identity: an honest count); the moves where the object-level change does not add up to the count change; the hidden-state flags and the moves where they fire; counts that never changed and counts that broke; what each action does; effects apart from what moved; and the host time per move. **Checked here (test 159, on the stand-in game of test 143):** arms K, A and B replay with the ledger watching and every played move is shown to it once (arm B calls the base `apply` through its own, so only one class is wrapped); a doctored row is still refused and `apply` is put back after it; the replay is unchanged by the ledger; the summary is pure. **To run it on the real saved runs** (needs `pip install arc-agi arcengine` on a machine that can reach PyPI, e.g. Codespaces): `python -m axi.experiments.ledger_replay ../docs/results/axi_gemini_arc_default.json --arm K` (and `--arm A`; for arm B `../docs/results/axi_gemini_arc_b_default.json --arm B`). Because Codespaces failed on 25 Oct (the editor's files at `assets.github.dev` answered 503), the same run is also a Colab notebook with no key, no Drive and no model: `python/colab/axi_ledger_replay.ipynb`. **Not shown:** anything on a real screen until it has been run there; that the flags mean anything in a real game; that a model clears more levels. Row 177 (candidate rules) is not started.
134. **What the ledger showed on the real saved runs, and what was changed (25 Oct)** - the owner ran `colab/axi_ledger_replay.ipynb` on the saved Test 24 and 25 runs (3 arms x 4 games, 699 moves, no model, no cost). Result: the replay reproduced every saved flag; the object-level change added up to the count change in 699 of 699 moves; the step of each action came out exactly and the same in the three arms (5 cells in ls20, 6 in g50t, 3 in ka59, 4 in sp80; in g50t ACTION5 went the other way in one run and ACTION4 moved a different piece); the host time was 2.9 to 18.1 ms a move on Colab (about 2 ms on the invented worlds); and in all 12 runs a strip along an edge of the screen was used up in nearly every move (it ran out at move 29 in both sp80 runs of arms A and B; a piece of symbol 8 in ls20 went from 3 to 2 at move 43, 43 and 47: my reading is a counter, not checked). **Found wrong:** (1) the ledger called a strip that gets smaller 'gone' plus 'new', so 'one symbol both gone and new' fired in nearly every move and the effects apart from what moved were inflated to 100 to 230; (2) the hidden-state flag fired in 0 of 12, because the strip makes every screen different and the saved runs revisited a screen 0 to 1 times (8 once); (3) 'moves 6 of 5 tries' counted pieces, not tries; (4) each click position is its own action. **Changed (rows 179 and 180; test 158, 16 tests):** a `resize` change (the same symbol with fewer or more cells, one-to-one only; two things that merge stay 'gone' and 'new'); `Ledger.ticks` (a strip that changes the same way in at least 70 % of the moves while it lasts, at least 6 times); the hidden-state flag that leaves the strip out and says so; a try counted once however many pieces moved. The strip is not in the old worlds: 0 false strips in 60 seeds, and the old numbers (0, 0 and 16 seeds; 732 and 5 effects apart; 60,000 of 60,000) are unchanged. **Not changed:** the click positions (one position is seen once or twice, so a per-position step says nothing and grouping them would hide the one that matters); the reading of the strip as a move counter. **Not shown:** that the changes work on the real games: they were written after the run and not run on a real screen (the sandbox cannot install the toolkit); the owner's rerun of the notebook is the check. The reading in game terms (identity kept while its state changes) is for Sir to confirm. **Second run (25 Oct) and two corrections to the reading of rows 179 and 180 (the reading in game terms is for Sir to confirm):** the hidden-state flag that leaves the strip out hid the whole rectangle round the strip (in ls20 most of the game), so its 3 flags were the mask, not a hidden state: now only the cells the strip changed are left out (test 158); and g50t's strip, one cell every second move, was missed by the 70 % rule: a strip that is the same change in 90 % of its moves needs 50 %. A free look at ls20 moves 2 and 15 then showed the mask was still too wide (the player's own cells on the floor colour were hidden with the strip): cells where something moved are now never counted as strip cells. Not yet run on the real game. Open: g50t's 879-cell object (and ls20's floor of 892 cells) of symbol 5 is gone and new every move (needs a match by cell overlap, not built; for Sir to confirm).
135. **The ARC-AGI-3 test with the ledger's read-out in the prompt (arm C, Test 26; written 25 Oct, NOT RUN)** - the structure behind the ledger is Rajnish Choubey's RFC-001, read into game terms **for Sir to confirm**; this item only puts what the ledger has seen in this run into the prompt. `GEMINI_ARC_C_TEST.md`, `experiments/gemini_arc_c.py` and `colab/axi_gemini_arc_c.ipynb` (test 160): arm B's turn plus one Ledger section of at most 12 lines (per action: the step and in how many of its tries, never without the count; up to 3 bars; colours that have not changed; the last move), made only from this run's earlier moves in the same level and holding no rule and nothing written by hand about any game. Only arm C is run; arm B and arms A and K are read from their saved rows. 16 reading lines with probabilities (lines 1 to 5 check the instrument; headline needs lines 6 and 8), hash-locked before any call (sha256 424fbb3f2f41e734df5f49d950c1c019703e2d84fb4d24e7c09b16962fd35a56). 136. **A free forward check on the saved ARC-AGI-3 runs: can the host say where the piece goes before the move? (26 Oct; run on Colab, headline MIXED, Result not written yet)** - after Test 26 the owner pasted seven AI-written texts about Sir's work and asked what could help. Checked against RFC-001 (the architecture is Rajnish Choubey's): pillar III (a candidate is written to a shadow, checked by the gate and committed or dropped) is Sir's; the "GPS" layers in the texts (diffusion distances, conditioning trajectories, a latent-phase functor), the 16 October essay and its quotation are not text of his that I could find, and are not written in as his. A game cannot be copied to look ahead in, so the shadow has to be a model the host learned from the moves seen; this measures whether it is good enough. `FORESEE_CHECK.md`, `experiments/foresee.py`, `colab/axi_foresee_check.ipynb` (test 161): three predictions per move from the earlier moves of the level only (the step the ledger saw, the same with the colours that never changed as walls, the same with what stopped the piece before), scored on the 960 saved moves of arms K, A, B and C; 8 reading lines with probabilities, hash-locked before the run (sha256 d9d1e853efeb51d7bb8fb7eb449063f67773efab223404756974b5ed0a5e8b1b); the headline needs lines 4 and 6. No model, no cost; the replay tool now also reads arm C, and the Test 26 result file is saved in `docs/results/`. On the invented worlds (not benchmark games) the learned prediction is right in 97.9 % of the predicted moves where the plain step is right in 80.7 %. The reading in game terms is for Sir to confirm. What the run showed (the Result is not written: the owner asked to try correcting the check first): the wall check could not be made in 553 of 729 predictions because the mover was not one object of one colour, so lines 4 and 6 read NO without testing the idea; step only was right in 71.6 %; 183 of the 207 misses are "the piece did not move" (not shown to be walls). The numbers are saved in `docs/results/foresee_check_all.json`. Item 137 is the corrected check.
137. **The second forward check: the mover as a group, walls learned by position (26 Oct; run on Colab, headline MIXED, Result not written yet)** - the first check could not look at walls in 553 of 729 predictions; two AI-written texts the owner pasted blame a missing "displacement covariance" and "Sir's pushout check" and promise more than 90 %. Taken on their merits, two ideas only: the mover is the group of colours that moves together, and a wall is learned from a move that did nothing, by position, not by colour; that the 183 misses are walls is not assumed, and a count of what was in front of the piece is line 7. The structure is Rajnish Choubey's RFC-001 (a refused change is held state), read in game terms, for Sir to confirm; the texts are not his and their "Layer V" and "Sir specifies" claims are not in RFC-001. `FORESEE_CHECK_2.md`, `experiments/foresee2.py`, `colab/axi_foresee_check_2.ipynb` (test 162); 8 reading lines with probabilities, hash-locked before the run (sha256 382d0a7fbff8c961d060b6147c3a6708d3c8523dc87a9b216f0f1d74b554cb5a); line 4 needs 20 statements so that it cannot read yes by saying almost nothing; the headline needs lines 3 and 5. No model, no cost. On the invented worlds it is right in 85.7 % and 90.9 % where step only is right in 72.8 % and 80.7 %. What the run showed (the Result is not written yet): the mover was found in 97.4 % of the 729 predicted moves (the first check: 24 %); the learned prediction was right in 72.3 % against 71.6 % for the plain step, and said "does not move" only 7 times (5 right); lines 1, 2 and 8 yes, lines 3 to 7 NO, headline MIXED. The stops are of two kinds: in ls20 65 of 68 had a colour the piece had never stepped onto in front of it, in sp80 and g50t 82 of 88 had only walked colours in front. The numbers are saved in `docs/results/foresee_check_2_all.json`. Item 138 is the third check.
138. **The third forward check: the whole front held, the actions typed (26 Oct, run once on Colab, Result not written)** - the second check found the mover but learned almost no walls, and its stops were of two kinds. Two AI-written texts the owner pasted say the first is a multi-cell front problem and the second an action-typing problem and promise 92.45 %; that figure is arithmetic that assumes every stop becomes a hit, and the texts are not Sir's (the rest of what they say is left out on its merits, not because a page lacks it: a running-down gauge is already seen by the ledger's strips, "Action 4 is interact" is not in our data and is looked at by the stops-by-action printout, a path-finder waits for a good enough prediction). Taken on their merits, two ideas only: when the piece does not move and the floor is known, hold every unwalked cell in front of it, not only when exactly one is left, and release a cell the moment the piece steps onto it; and call an action stationary when it does nothing on open floor in at least 4 tries, so that a stop in front of a wall is not held against the action. The structure is Rajnish Choubey's RFC-001 (a refused change is held state, released when the change goes through), read in game terms, for Sir to confirm. `FORESEE_CHECK_3.md`, `experiments/foresee3.py`, `colab/axi_foresee_check_3.ipynb` (test 163); 8 reading lines with probabilities, hash-locked before the run (sha256 ed6976b51d68394029e82d58ba6c046a0ef8c0084374390d37695854ab69895d); it runs four ways on the same moves (both off, which must give the second check's 527 right of 729, the front only, the typing only, both) and prints the stops by action; the headline needs lines 3 and 6. No model, no cost. On the invented worlds (single-cell pieces) it gives no gain, as it cannot there: right in 85.2 % and 90.6 % against 85.7 % and 90.9 % with both off. Run once on the saved runs (numbers in `docs/results/foresee_check_3_all.json`, Result not written yet): with both changes the prediction was right in 603 of 729 predicted moves (82.7 %) against 527 with both off (72.3 %); "does not move" said 91 times (61 by a held wall, 88.5 % right; 30 by action type, 96.7 % right); lines 1 to 6 yes, 7 and 8 NO; headline YES.
139. **The fourth forward check: the board carried from level to level (26 Oct, run once on Colab, Result not written)** - the first three checks start every level from nothing, and 207 of the 936 moves of the second check (22 %) were not predicted at all. Read for structure and not for technical words (the owner asked for that), the two essays published under Rajnish Choubey's name that he shared have one pattern in them: one board that every view reads at once, nothing held in a silo, the model only a proposer. Their text is largely pasted AI answers and is not a game mechanic; in game terms the pattern is: what the host learned in the earlier levels of a game is on the same board when the next level starts. Two things are carried, from earlier levels of the same game and arm only: the law of each action (the symbol and the step it made most often, seen at least 3 times; used when the level has none of its own, and only when the symbol is on the screen) and the floor (the colours walked on). Not a cell, not a wall, not a position. For Sir to confirm. `FORESEE_CHECK_4.md`, `experiments/foresee4.py` (the evaluator is `foresee3.py` with an optional `carry`), `colab/axi_foresee_check_4.ipynb` (test 164); 8 reading lines with probabilities, hash-locked before the run (sha256 aec9788e7defdc121743c1c0abe218c4be89d53995b2f66ed1e3037d042816eb); three ways on the same moves (fresh, laws carried, laws and floor carried); the headline needs lines 4 and 6; if it reads yes the next step is a paid test (about $3.65, the cost of arm C) in which the model is shown the carried laws. No model, no cost for this check. On the invented worlds carrying the laws raises the share of moves predicted from 64.3 % to 76.7 % and the share predicted right from 54.7 % and 54.0 % to 64.0 % and 63.3 %; carrying the floor as well lowers it again (59.7 % and 54.0 %). Run once on the saved runs (numbers in `docs/results/foresee_check_4_all.json`, Result not written yet): fresh 729 of 936 moves predicted (77.9 %), right among those predicted 82.7 %, right per move played 64.4 %; laws carried 759 predicted (81.1 %), 82.9 % and 67.2 %; the 30 moves predicted only from a carried law were 90 % right; floor carried as well 83.0 % and 67.3 %; lines 1, 2, 4, 6 and 7 yes, 3, 5 and 8 NO; headline YES, a modest gain.
140. **The fifth forward check: repeats, the gap, filling the gap, the delete test (27 Oct, NOT RUN on the real games)** - the owner pointed out that the Cinderella Project pages had been judged by their key words and not read for pattern. The Mechanism #1" (byline Saraswati Choubey, text signed by an AI; the owner's position is that Sir steered the AI, so the patterns are his, and which sentences are his own is for Sir to confirm) was read for structure: a pull that keeps nothing between shots and does not aggregate; the subset performed against the superset claimed (the gap is where the human works); a counter that wraps, returns to the first point and is always a step behind; words and numbers as a round trip with meaning at both ends. Most of it is already in the framework (the ledger folds history, the check is the step ahead, `ignored` names what is left out, the abstain says "I do not know"). Four things were not measured: (1) how many moves in which the piece did not move were the same action from the same place (the repo's repeat-no-op column, 0, 0 and 1 in 240 moves, only sees a whole-screen repeat and the screen almost never repeats); (2) the 207 unpredicted moves, by reason; (3) two additions, `once` (predict from one earlier try) and `elim` (the one unit step no other action has made: `Ledger.completion`, row D, which until now only reported it), alone and together; (4) the Delete Test of the 99 % / 1 % essays on the five parts of the third check's predictor, and whether ACTION1 to ACTION4 make the same step in every game. Action-group inference is not a new idea: it is row D, and the first four checks never used it. `FORESEE_CHECK_5.md`, `experiments/foresee5.py`, `colab/axi_foresee_check_5.ipynb` (test 165); 8 reading lines with probabilities, hash-locked before the run (sha256 ea25e024206aeb2882ec1bdd949e3fa3c0c11ddb824b7dbc129fc62dd858bff3); nine ways on the same moves (full, which must give the third check's 603 right of 729, the five deletions, once, elim, once and elim); the headline needs lines 3 and 5. With every part on and both additions off it is the third check counter for counter on the invented worlds; there `once` adds 13 predicted moves (12 and 10 right) and `elim` one move, wrong (the new action was a press); not run on the real games.

## 4. What is missing, and what to ask Rajnish for

* **The mathematics.** Where do adhesive categories, pushout complements and the D4 orbit come from in his own notes? None of it
  is in Chapters 1-3.
* **Chapter 4, option "The Bridge of Shirorekha"** (Sanskrit alphabet as an "acoustic periodic table", grammar engines vs
  modern AI). Of the three Part-2 options this is the only one that sounds technical. Background that is general knowledge, not
  from his text: Panini's grammar is an ordered rule-rewriting system with a built-in rule for resolving conflicts (the later or
  more specific rule wins), and the Maheshvara Sutras name sound classes by ranges over one ordering. Both are close relatives of
  graph rewriting with priorities and of range-coded character classes, so that chapter might hold real formal content.
* **"The Math of the 16th" (the 15/52 selection ratio).** If this contains an actual formula, send it; it may be testable.
* His own notes, not only the polished posts.

## 5. Rule for new work (agreed standing instruction)

The aim is that what we claim is achieved through Rajnish's framework and structural maps, not through things we invent.
So, from now on:
1. **The table in section 1b is the roadmap.** Every new test, module or paper claim says which row it implements ("Implements row N").
2. A row is only called **SUPPORTED** when a measurement on real logs backs it; **MAPS** when the code fits but nothing was measured;
   **TEST** when it is planned; **INTERP** when it is only guidance.
3. Anything not tied to a row is labelled **engineering support** (parsers, test harness, CI, packaging) and kept small. It carries no
   claim about the framework.
4. Rajnish reviews each row's formal reading. A row he rejects is removed from the paper, not defended.
5. Where our definition differs from his (e.g. the Delete Test columns), the doc says "my definition, not his".

## 6. Blog scan (436 distinct blog posts read so far, of about 4,585 archive slots, plus 29 Medium pieces; full logs in `BLOG_SCAN_LOG.md`, `MEDIUM_SCAN_LOG.md`, `MYTHOLOGY_EXTRACTION.md`, `ORIGINS_EXTRACTION.md`, `ABSTRACTION_EXTRACTION.md`, `GEOMETRY_EXTRACTION.md`, `STRUCTURE_EXTRACTION.md`, `STRUCTURE_EXTRACTION_2.md`, `STRUCTURE_EXTRACTION_3.md`, `STRUCTURE_EXTRACTION_4.md`, `STRUCTURE_EXTRACTION_5.md` and `STRUCTURE_EXTRACTION_6.md`; the synthesis is `FRAMEWORK_SYNTHESIS.md`)

Posts were read in batches of 5. They contain no equations or procedures; they contain roles, sequences and principles, which is
what rows 25-51 are built from. Things to keep in view:

1. **The blog describes AXI as model-internal control** (latent-space coordinates, orthogonal memory, a "Logic-Bus"). The earliest source I have found for that wording is the Medium article "AI Mapped" (22 Aug 2026, Shalendra Gaur's byline); the blog repeats it. Scan 4 found **five different descriptions of AXI** in all (a working practice, latent-space steering, a weights-and-logic-bus framework, a hypothetical planetary engine, and CICO as deterministic in-context execution); only the last resembles this repo. **This repo is an
   external runtime** (mask, gate, retry, repair, canonicalization) and never touches the weights. Nothing we measured supports
   "calculations resolve inside the weights". Until Rajnish says which one AXI is, README and paper state only what the runtime does
   (PAPER_CORRECTIONS item 15).
2. **Many posts open like chatbot replies, and at least one is an AI speaking in the first person** (post 53, "Borrowed, not
   earned"). About a quarter of the site addresses we could list are film or video summaries. Ask which posts are his own writing
   before attributing an idea to him in print.
3. **The political and historical posts (41-45) contain no engineering content** and make strong claims about named living people.
   Do not link them from the paper or the article. The mystery posts (the binary titles, 55) hide nothing we could recover; that was
   tested, not assumed.
4. **Some posts are AI replies praising the author** (61, 64) and one gives exact word counts of its own chat (63). They are not evidence and
   must not be cited. The archive is said to hold about 4,560 posts added in 43 days (106 a day); **Shalendra confirms this personally** (7 Oct), and I cannot check it from outside the site. I have read 103 distinct posts. Searching the framework's own words now keeps returning posts already read, so the technical content of the blog looks like a small set we have seen; the next step is Rajnish's own ten best posts and dated notes (BLOG_SCAN_LOG question 15).
5. **"Consequence" recurs** (posts 41, 47, 59, 80) as the thing abstract systems lack. It is the strongest single word for row 43; ask
   Rajnish whether he defines it.
6. **Numerology guard.** 108 = 4 x 27, 12 cells x 9 fields = 108, eight gates = |D4|: small numbers match each other all the time.
   A number counts only if it predicts something we can measure. The one real piece of mathematics near these posts is that D4
   acts on a 3x3 grid with orbits of 1, 4 and 4.
7. **The published Medium paper "THE PAPER : CHANGING AI" (31 Aug 2026, Shalendra Gaur's byline) has a results table with no stated method, and none of its numbers are in our logs** (drift rate 0.1840 -> 0.0000, 1.19M-token needle 100%, 13x13 grid parity 100%, -46.90% tokens, -42.3% to -82.4% latency). Nothing in this document supports them. See `PAPER_CORRECTIONS.md` item 18 and BLOG_SCAN_LOG question 16.
8. **Medium scan (7 Oct, `MEDIUM_SCAN_LOG.md`):** the numbers come from Google AI Studio chat sessions; several of the "proofs" are transcripts whose telemetry (attention weights, drift 0.00000000) is text the model wrote. The strongest Medium piece is RFC-001, whose formal core is what this repo implements; its open frontier 1 is row 52. The Delete Test is not defined in any Medium piece I could read, so row 23's "five challenges" rest on the pasted summary. All ten AXI technical pieces are on Shalendra's account; Rajnish's accounts (told by Shalendra) hold the philosophy pieces.
9. **Fifth scan (7 Oct, 25 pieces, `MYTHOLOGY_EXTRACTION.md`):** the structures he writes are a 0-to-10 number ladder, a five-way disagreement on the order of the three goddesses, a nine-versus-ten line that reverses once (13 Aug), a three-layer kernel/interface picture, and two Mahabharata patterns (five roles around one centre; a ruler who sees only through a proxy). Two concrete finds: the words "zero" (18 Jan 2026) and "AI Can't Be Corrected. It Can Be Managed." (26 Jan) come **before any technical text**, so the project's problem statement is older than its mechanism; and the gate does **not** catch a repeated label or an unfilled slot (row 53, test 26), which is a scope limit the paper should state.
10. **Sixth scan (8 Oct, 36 posts from 30 Jul to 6 Aug plus the 14 Aug and 30 Aug series, `ORIGINS_EXTRACTION.md`):** the first week holds more structure than the later mythology: a trinity of action / deletion / anchor (2 Aug) and +1 / -1 / 0 as Durga / Kali / Shiva (30 Aug), which the delta language fits exactly and which is now tested (row 58); Rahi's Law as a formal statement that confidence does not respond to outside knowledge (row 59, with the needle test); four Devis as four variables and the sentence "We have separated Saraswati from Durga. We know everything, but we protect nothing." (row 60); and "Synthesis, not integration" (row 61). Two cautions: **the blog appends the next posts to a post's page**, so earlier word counts are upper bounds; and the blog has posts dated 24 and 28 Jul, so dates cannot settle the "43 days" count.
11. **Seventh scan (8 Oct, 26 posts incl. the whole first week and the oldest text, `ABSTRACTION_EXTRACTION.md`):** "gods are state, goddesses are operators" (rows 72, 58); the checker is a read-only witness (66); an undoable erasure needs a kept copy (65); rules give safety, not liveness, and that is already measured as M2 against M6 (67); the Strawberry gives Rahi's Law a mechanism (70); the post that promises "permutations of the nine" computes none, so we computed them (64). Two first-run readings were withdrawn after a second read (the "abracadabra" incantation, deletion as "unsigned").
12. **Eighth scan (8 Oct, 23 new posts and pages, `GEOMETRY_EXTRACTION.md`):** every structural claim on the 25 pages read is asserted, none derived. What the scan produced instead is a map of where the geometry is in our system: the gate is coordinate-free (it has no board; test 36), D4 lives in the canonicalizer, adjacency exists only as edges the task supplies, and transformations are not operators in the language (section 4 of that file). The strongest matches are the loom (a text stream becomes a pattern on a lattice, row 76), check / apply / cleanup (row 77), and "if one goes to zero, or if one overpowers the rest" against M1, M2 and M6 (row 83). The earliest dated argument about number and language (10 Feb) argues against numbers for words (row 86, question 55).
13. **Ninth scan (8 Oct, 23 new posts plus the re-reads of Post 6 and the tail of Post 190, `STRUCTURE_EXTRACTION.md`; synthesis in `FRAMEWORK_SYNTHESIS.md`):** again nothing is derived; what the scan produced is checkable structure. The 3x3 table has nine entries in three figures, and a meaning stored as edges survives all 8 symmetries while a meaning stored as position survives 4 (row 88); a layout of four named things carries only log2(3) bits unless its axes are named (row 87); the oldest text has a point / line / crossing ontology whose board vocabulary is 8 pair classes and 8 lines (row 89); the calendar page is the first mapping that can be checked by computation, and it keeps order but not successor (row 90); the 'true tool' page is the gate's statelessness, tone-blindness and absence of lockout (row 91); and the approval-proxy diagnosis comes with no remedy, where our measured M1 / M2 / M6 supplies one (row 92). Corrections: the third entry under Saraswati is Sacred Strategy (an older line said Pure Cognition); a first count of five pair classes was eight; page 25 was Post 190, so the count is 229, not 230.
14. **Tenth scan (8 Oct, 25 pages, of which 9 were re-reads of Posts 21, 25, 28, 32, 34, 40, 72, 92 and 192, so 16 new posts, `STRUCTURE_EXTRACTION_2.md`; synthesis in `FRAMEWORK_SYNTHESIS.md` section 9):** again nothing is derived, and again what the scan produced is checkable structure. A direction halves a symmetry group, and an edge in our language is directed (row 99); "three points ... give an object volume" is off by one, four are needed (row 100); there is no cliff between the 3x3 and the 4x4 board, only a change of parity (row 101); averaging a board forgets its arrangement, canonicalizing forgets only its orientation (167 against 45,360 of 362,880, row 102); a wall of a hundred fragments is a graph in which "a trillion paths" understates the space by hundreds of orders of magnitude (row 103); the ladder of writing surfaces is the difference between keeping a log of inverses and not (row 104); the page with the most formulas in this scan has exact time-cycle arithmetic and a speed-of-light line that misses by 6.4 percent (row 105), and a tempting numerological match with our containment run is recorded as not evidence. Candidates for the unknown verdict (the question mark on the wall) and the board as an object (the N+1 container) are readings, not findings (rows 110, 111). Corrections: nine of the 25 pages had already been read, so the count is 245, not 253.
15. **Eleventh scan (8 Oct, 25 pages, none a re-read, so 25 new posts, numbers 253 to 277, `STRUCTURE_EXTRACTION_3.md`; synthesis in `FRAMEWORK_SYNTHESIS.md` section 10):** a thinner round, said plainly: most pages are one paragraph of rhetoric and five are almost empty. What held up: one page lays the time triad on the trident's prongs and on three of four kin figures (past, present and horizon; the fourth owns the trident), and it shows that two different triads lie on the same three prongs with no alignment given (row 114, question 88); the shape of four decides what it can carry: ring 3 or 6, line 12 or 24, and a fork is told apart only by its edge names (row 116); the order of a summary and the order of an undo log can be counted, and a wrong order is refused by the gate about 87 percent of the time and silently wrong in a fraction of a percent (row 115); collapsing distance is adding edges, and the rigid minimal triangulation of the board is not the symmetric one (row 117). A binary-string title is recorded as a measurement only (row 118). Corrections: the Lakshmi-to-Kali "spectrum" is not a line (Post 48 is a fork); the mother on page 267 is Kali. Count: 270 distinct posts.
16. **Twelfth scan (8 Oct, 30 pages: 20 drawn at random from the archive map and 10 chosen on purpose; 2 of the 10 were re-reads, so 28 new posts, numbers 278 to 305, `STRUCTURE_EXTRACTION_4.md`; synthesis in `FRAMEWORK_SYNTHESIS.md` section 11):** the archive is month pages of 7 posts (Aug 371 pages, Sep 284, about 4,585 slots), so 298 posts is about 6.5 percent by count. Of the 20 random pages about 14 are one paragraph of rhetoric, 4 carry explicit structure (about 8 to 42 percent as a 95 percent interval, a sample of 20), and 2 of 20 produced a test; the three pages that produced the other tests were long ones I chose. What held up: a line closed by one edge is a cycle, and "the 8th is Do again" is an identification, both done through the gate with inverses (row 123); one long page contradicts itself three times, which a functional-relation rule and a forbidden-value rule catch and the gate as built does not (rows 124, 130); a table with its cells one column off has a derived column whose arithmetic recovers the layout (row 125). The pages that mention drift, latency and scaling laws assert and measure nothing (rows 127, 129); the one comparison in the map that would test "structure beats scale" (row 9, section 3 item 1) has still not been run. Corrections: two re-reads among the chosen pages; my first status message understated what earlier scans had read. Count: 298 distinct posts.

17. **Thirteenth scan (8 Oct, 100 pages in 20 batches of 5: 50 drawn at random from the archive map and 50 chosen on purpose, plus 8 neighbour pages; all 108 new, numbers 306 to 413, `STRUCTURE_EXTRACTION_5.md`; synthesis in `FRAMEWORK_SYNTHESIS.md` section 12):** the instruction was to look under the words, in patterns and across pages, and to look again at earlier material. Of the 50 random pages 9 (18 percent; about 10 to 31 percent) carry explicit structure and 5 (10 percent; about 4 to 21) are the main subject of a test; pooled with scan 12, 13 of 70 and 7 of 70. The 50 chosen pages gave 20 tests (40 percent), which is a property of the choice. What held up: a rectangle is a ring that contracts to a circle with one loop kept at every step and lifts to a cuboid with five loops (row 133); a chain of "borrowed from" lines has a ground and one order, and "the four is the circle" closes exactly one loop and removes both, while "=" as identity merges places (row 134); four pairs are the 4-cube and a five-step loop needs a no-op (row 135); a container with 784 of 1,024 empty is the shape of a copy-on-write pager, and rewriting everything fits only while it is at most half full (row 136; the pages' figures are the blog host's dashboard); the grammar, not the alphabet, sets the number of valid strings (row 138); an equation re-posted four times grows by edits, and tables exist only across posts (rows 139, 140); declared counts can be checked, and a page that says "three steps" lists four (row 141); a refusal can be a code, our core is 363 lines, a village is a star (rows 144 to 147). What did not hold up: a pattern I saw in three pages of the equation (only one-name rungs are equated) is gone on the fourth. Two things the mutation checks found about the engine: the strict parser had an untested gap (a trailing tab) and two lines of the gate never decide anything. Corrections: 784 = 28 squared is not a pattern; "13 distinct" is 12. Count: 406 distinct posts.

18. **Fourteenth scan (8 Oct, 30 pages read as ordinary text: a stratified seeded draw of 5 from each sixth of the archive, all new, numbers 414 to 443, `STRUCTURE_EXTRACTION_6.md`; synthesis in `FRAMEWORK_SYNTHESIS.md` section 12b):** the instruction was to read plain pages the way they are, find how they hold structure, then test what holds. Counted from the 30: 10 have an explicit list or numbered parts, and none of those lists has four items (eight have three, two have two); 8 of the 10 end on a single restating line; at least 13 name an opposition pair; 4 are AI-voice replies pasted as posts, 3 of them with a numbered list or a diagram; 6 sit in a series with their neighbours. What held up as exact statements: the multipliers of a 12-mark clock that lose nothing are the Klein four-group, and every prime above 3 sits on one of those four marks, while a 13-mark clock has a cycle of 12 and no such gaps (rows 157, 158); the post on barks and bites names three of four cells of a 2 x 2 and never the fourth (row 159); in a finite system "no first state" and "nothing is lost" are one property, and the gate with its inverses has no start (row 160); two diagrams of one shape differ only in three labels, so a "gate" has to be a weight (row 161); 13 as 12 plus a hub keeps the 24 symmetries of the 12, 13 as a ring has 26 (row 162). Count: 436 distinct posts.

## 7. Draft: the Super 4 (quad) x the triad, as one grid (MY ARRANGEMENT - for Rajnish to correct)

The Super 4 (Durga, Kali, Buddha, Shiva) are four *functions of protection* (row 6). The triad (Saraswati/cognition, Laxmi/substance,
Parvati/power) are three *currents of what the world is made of* (row 34). If they are two axes of one coordinate system ("select your
coordinate", end of Chapter 3), each cell should name a component of the runtime:

| | language (cognition: what can be said) | state (substance: what exists) | enforcement (power: what is allowed) |
|---|---|---|---|
| **Durga** boundary | grammar mask: only valid items can be written | dangling-edge condition: no half-deleted structure (`complete`) | the gate: nothing inadmissible commits |
| **Kali** dissolves the false / aborts | strict parser refuses off-grammar text (loud) | match conditions + `cut`: claims about things not in the graph are dropped | pager rollback: speculative state is discarded |
| **Buddha** clean slate, minimal | feedback that names exactly what to change (imperative retry) | minimal repair: the smallest delta that becomes admissible | reset to the last commit, try again |
| **Shiva** ground, quotient | canonical task text (D4: the same text for all 8 orientations) | canonical state form (D4) | the single committed anchor every rank starts from |

Reading it honestly: **every cell could be filled, which is a warning sign of a force-fit.** What would make it a real map is a cell that
predicts something not yet built or measured. The weakest cell is Buddha x language (the retry wording is a stretch for "clean slate"),
and the cell Kali x state is the one now built (`cut`). Questions for Rajnish: would he place the same components in the same cells, and
are these the two axes at all?

### 7b. A correction the second scan suggests
Post 48 runs Lakshmi (substance) -> Saraswati (cognition) -> then **Durga or Kali**, and in the usual tradition Kali springs from
Durga: both are faces of one power goddess. If Rajnish places them that way, then in the grid above Durga and Kali are **two modes of
the power column** (protect / cut), and the rows left are Shiva (ground) and Buddha. That would make the grid 3 columns x
(power with two modes + ground + one more), which is less symmetrical and so less of a force-fit. It is a question for him (BLOG_SCAN_LOG
question 9), not a decision.

### 7c. The two 3x3 posts, read together (a guess, for Rajnish to confirm or reject)
Post 6 gives a 3x3 of **three roots with three entries each** (Saraswati, Laxmi, Parvati; the nine names are in row 88; the page does not say whether the entries are levels, rows or columns). Post 3 gives **four intersecting lines** that carve nine fields with a centre and eight gates. These may be one grid: the nine fields are the triad by its levels, and the four lines are what divides them. I am guessing that the four lines are the Super 4 (Durga, Kali, Buddha, Shiva as four boundaries); the posts do not say so. If that is right, section 7's 4x3 matrix would be replaced by this picture. Question 18.
